美股擺脫通膨疑慮，利率仍偏震盪格局
2026 年 6 月
5 月截至 22 日為止，全球股市表現(以美元計價)呈現高度分化，「AI 半導體」與「油價地緣政
治」兩股力道拉鋸，使各區域報酬差距明顯擴大。MSCI 新興亞洲與新興市場期間分別勁揚 7.1%與
5.4%，雙雙領先，主要受惠台灣與南韓在 AI 半導體供應鏈的強勁買盤 ，南韓今年漲幅已逾八成、
台灣約四成，為全球最熱的股市。成熟市場方面，標普 500 期間上漲 3.8%，MSCI 日本、歐洲分別
上漲 3%與 1.8%，均有不錯表現。相對地，MSCI 拉丁美洲單月下跌 4.3%，為主要市場中表現最弱，

主因年初大漲後，通膨上揚壓縮巴西後續降息空間及該國的大選選情變化導致投資人獲利了結。中國
與印度表現不佳，大致是受美伊衝突帶動國際油價走高的拖累。
相較於 4 月在美元走弱帶動下的全面反彈，5 月截至 22 日為止，全球債市明顯承壓，七大類債
券中六類收黑，主要仍是因美伊衝突推升國際油價，再度點燃通膨疑慮，全球債券殖利率走升所致。
以券種觀察，存續期間較長、利率敏感度較高的全球主權債券單月下跌  0.85%、全球投資級公司債
也下跌 0.47%；新興當地債亦由４月的領漲轉為小幅回檔，反映美元自５月中旬開始轉強，導致匯
率貢獻由正轉負。 亞洲高收益債則逆勢上漲  0.33%，為唯一收紅 券種，主要受惠較高的票息收益
（carry）與相對較短的存續期間，於殖利率走升環境中提供緩衝。整體而言，在地緣與通膨變數明
朗化之前，債市配置宜留意存續期間風險以強化防禦性。
儘管中東戰事推升通膨疑慮，但自 4 月起以來，原油、美股走向明顯分歧。美股在 AI 資本支出
題材與強勁財報帶動下改寫歷史新高，與過去地緣衝突常見的 「油漲股跌」模式背道而馳。美伊談判

進展反覆或使股市短期波動，但美國 11 月期中選舉將迫使川普在中東議題作出妥協，市場的通膨疑
慮可望下降，且 AI 題材對科技股的盈餘帶動也料持續，科技類股與台日股市都仍有向上潛力。
債市方面，美伊和談雖正向發展，但因雙方歧見仍存，短期內殖利率料維持高檔震盪。然而，目
前債券殖利率水準明顯高於歷史均值，我們認為站在息收角度，債券仍具長線投資價值。此外，投資
人對美債殖利率曲線陡峭化疑慮短期不會消退，加上長債存續期間偏高。因此，我們仍維持債券投資
聚焦中短天期公債/投資級債的建議。

美國股市：市場樂觀預期美伊協議達成，美股再創新高
五月美股聚焦於 AI 產業進展與供應鏈訊息，且投資人樂觀預期美伊有望達成協議，並使荷姆茲
海峽恢復正常通行， 推動標普與納指 再創歷史新高 。指數上揚主要由 蘋果、美光、超微半導體 、
Alphabet、特斯拉、禮來、輝達、英特爾、思科等科技股帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括 Meta、微
軟、沃爾瑪、萬事達卡、直覺、網飛、埃克森美孚、碩騰、新紀元能源、康菲石油等，市場焦點回歸
AI 贏家與受惠的供應鏈。
美國 5 月製造業 PMI 自上月的 54.5 上升至 55.3，服務業 PMI 自上月的 51.0 下降至 50.9，低於

市場預期的 51.2。企業活動維持溫和擴張，但產業表現分化，其中製造業受到企業補庫存帶動，而
服務業略微放緩，高物價與不確定性持續壓抑消費與企業支出意願。此外，就業市場同樣出現分化，
製造業就業小幅增加，但整體民間企業聘僱增速下降，企業招聘態度趨於保守。價格方面，供應鏈延
誤與能源價格上漲推升企業成本。綜合來看，雖然美國經濟的確具有韌性，但通膨壓力仍持續累積。
美國 5 月密大消費者信心指數顯示，由於高油價與通膨疑慮升溫，消費者信心再創新低。密大消
費者信心指數終值由初值的 48.2 下修至 44.8，現況指標由初值的 47.8 下修至 45.8，預期指標則由
48.5 下滑至 44.1。消費者 1 年期通膨預期上升至 4.8%，且 5-10 年中長期通膨預期則大幅升至 3.9%，
全面高於預期。油價與生活成本壓力持續惡化下，消費者不僅對當前經濟與財務狀況評價惡化，對未
來景氣與通膨前景亦轉趨悲觀。近期 FOMC 會議紀錄顯示部分官員已開始討論移除政策聲明中的寬
鬆傾向指引，反映聯準會內部同樣對通膨風險的警戒升溫，政策立場轉趨偏鷹。

AI 投資熱潮推動標普 500 企業現金配置從庫藏股轉向資本支出 。 市場共識預估AI 雲端業者(亞馬
遜、Alphabet、META、微軟、甲骨文)2026 年資本支出可望超過 7,000 億美元，為強化現金流量，
這些公司只好削減庫藏股。近期財報季已突顯由庫藏股轉向資本支出與研發的趨勢，大型科技巨擘除
非選擇大規模舉債，否則未來一年企業庫藏股動能恐難明顯回升，讓未來一年的股市波動性擴大。
目前標普 500 本益比已升至約 26 倍，前瞻本益比也在 22 倍位置，評價面已偏高，加上新任聯
準會主席就任後政策不確定性、美伊談判反覆、AI 主題擁擠度偏高等多重變數，市場拉回風險仍需
留意。然而基本面仍具支撐，故雖然短期需考量估值、通膨與地緣政治風險，但中長期則維持對美股
及 AI 產業正面看法，操作上建議以分批、長期角度布局。

日本股市：AI 題材提振日股表現；日本央行 6 月升息機率上升
五月日股受惠於 AI 產業表現強勁，日經 225 指數站上 6 萬 5 千點歷史新高。市場主要由軟銀、
村田製作所、瑞可利控股、瑞薩電子、鎧俠、東京威力科創、三菱 UFJ 金融、索尼、住友商事等公
司帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括三菱重工、愛德萬測試、藤倉、三井物產、豪雅、丸紅、任天堂、
豐田、Disco、富士通等。市場焦點回歸 AI 主題，以及升息可能帶動的金融股。
日本 4 月出口年增 14.8%。弱勢日圓對出口帶來挹注，而實質出口年增 3.4%、連續兩個月維持

正成長，顯示出口量的成長亦是推動出口上行因素之一 。引導出口成長的重要品項包含半導體電子零
組件及非鐵金屬，其中半導體等電子零組件連續八個月的雙位數成長，就是受惠於 AI 產業需求。
日本 4 月 CPI 年增幅由前月的 1.5%下降至 1.4%，扣除新鮮食品的 CPI 年增率則由 1.8%上升至
1.4%，本月通膨的走弱為政策性補貼所影響。其中商品價格年增率上升至 1.7%，服務價格年增率則
降至 0.9%。展望後市，日本政府很可能繼續以財政手段平滑 CPI 波動。首相高市早苗於 5/25 宣布，
為因應長期化的能源成本飆漲 ， 日本政府將編列規模約為3 兆日圓的追加預算案，實際內容包括汽油
補貼、電力及瓦斯費補貼。高市同時承諾不會增加整體政府借貸，試圖緩解財政壓力憂慮。
貨幣政策方面，市場預期日銀將在 6 月啟動升息。近期日銀委員小枝淳子談話，提及應以適當地
速度調升政策利率以因應物價上漲，認為中東局勢惡化導致物價上漲的風險正在增加，然而小枝淳子
於上月會議認為應維持政策利率不變。先前美國財長貝森特表示，日本經濟基本面強勁，日圓過度波

動並不可取，相信日本央行將會採取必要行動。市場預期日本央行於 6 月啟動升息(下次會議時間為
6/10~6/11)，全年至少有 2 碼升息空間。近期可觀察日本十年期公債殖利率飆升來到 2.7%，對照美
國十年期公債殖利率仍在 4.5%，雙方利差持續縮減，有利日圓守穩 160 關卡。
日本 4 月外國人入境人數年減 5.5%至 369.2 萬人次，中國訪日旅客年減 56.8%至 33.1 萬人，連
續五個月出現雙位數的衰退，反映自去年日中關係惡化，中國遊客數維持於 30 萬人左右區間，但整
體外籍觀光客仍在單月 350~380 萬人次的高檔位置，顯示對日本觀光產業造成的負面影響有限。
日本經濟受惠於 AI 產業擴張，加上日本政府對 17 項戰略產業政策支撐明確，然而股市短期面對
中東衝突不確定性及漲多拉回風險，建議投資人可留意中東情勢 、 補充預算審議進度及日銀6 月會議
釋放的政策訊號，把握利空拉回機會、以長線角度、分批佈局日股。

新興亞洲股市：半導體與油價角力，亞洲市場分化表現
亞洲股市 5 月因中東情勢的和緩在科技類股的帶領下反彈，截至 5/26，亞洲不含日本指數
(MXAPJ) 過去一個月上漲 5.59%。南韓股市漲幅居前，KOSPI 指數單月上漲 21.19%。台灣加權指
數表現居次，上漲 12.10%。中國股市表現平平，上證指數上漲 1.78%，國企指數不漲反跌，下跌
2.56%。MSCI 東協指數受惠於美伊情勢和緩，5 月上漲 2.15%，但各國表現不盡相同。原本受惠於
油價上漲的馬來西亞及印尼由漲轉跌，其中印尼股市遭到外資短時間內在股市大舉提款，股匯雙殺，

單月下跌 12.95%，亞股中最差。新加坡、泰國、菲律賓則有 3-6%的漲幅。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌
0.55%，印度作為能源淨進口國，5 月上旬高企的國際油價加劇了市場對其國內通膨的擔憂，使得印
度央行在貨幣政策操作上的降息空間受到壓制，也壓抑了股市。
5 月亞洲製造業呈現以科技出口為核心的兩極分化格局。台灣在 AI 算力與全球半導體供應鏈強
勁拉動下，PMI 飆升至 60.3 領跑全亞洲；南韓則在外需復甦與記憶體晶片需求帶動下展開 V 型反
彈，一路回升至 53.6。印度雖自高檔拉回至 54.3，經濟在高油價的干擾下雖能維持擴張，但卻連續
放緩 。東協因歐美消費降溫 、 通膨壓制 ， 動能連月放緩至 50.7 的榮枯線邊緣； 中國大陸則持續在 50.3
微幅擴張處橫盤拉鋸，反映整體內需復甦依舊沉悶。
亞洲經濟呈現典型的 「科技強、傳統弱」格局，台、韓、兩國受惠於結構性轉型紅利而氣勢如虹，
雖也共同面臨中東衝突引發的原物料與運費飆升等成本通膨隱憂，但影響有限；相反地，缺乏科技大
潮加持的東協、印度與中國，在全球高利率與需求放緩的環境下，正提早面臨景氣停滯的嚴峻挑戰。

台、韓兩國經濟擴張具韌性，顯示全球算力基礎建設正處於實質出貨期。由於地緣政治與中東情
勢陰霾未散，資金將更傾向流入具有「實質獲利高成長」與「定價能力」的東北亞半導體巨頭。台股
加權指數與南韓 KOSPI 的強勢格局具備基本面支撐，高昂的運費與原物料成本雖是隱憂，但先進製
程與高階記憶體具備將成本轉嫁給歐美科技巨擘的能力。印度 PMI 持續放緩，股市將受高油價壓制，
印度的核心邏輯在於 「長線結構性轉型」 。未來只要中東地緣情勢未進一步惡化，國際油價能逐步回
穩，印度央行的貨幣政策擔憂將減輕。預期印度股市在震盪調整之後，長線仍有投資機會。中國製造
業 PMI 與股市的橫盤疲軟顯示內需消費信心的提振需要更多時間。未來可能的發展仍維持「以時間
換取空間」的築底格局，指數上漲空間有限。

科技類股：AI 資本支出全線擴散，企業獲利上行週期確立多頭韌性
全球科技板塊在 AI 基礎設施資本支出持續投入的背景下，正經歷結構性的架構轉型與產業鏈整
體獲利及估值上調。從大型雲端 CSP 業者的長期資本支出規劃，到硬體、網通與半導體設備大廠的
板塊效應外溢，均顯示 AI 需求具有實質支撐。儘管總體經濟環境面臨美債殖利率上行與高利率環境
的挑戰 ， 但科技類股目前正處於明確的獲利上行週期 ，企業獲利的增長預期將有效緩解估值修正壓力 。
根據彭博智庫與 S&P Global 的最新預測，全球大型雲端業者在 2026 年的總資本支出預計將達

到 8,200 億美元，較先前預估的 7,500 億美元出現顯著上修。在 2026 至 2030 年的長線格局下，全
球六大領先 CSP 業者的累計 AI 資本支出將接近 5 兆美元。其中，亞馬遜在 2026 年的資本支出預計
以 2,000 億美元位居首位，微軟與 Alphabet 緊隨其後。雖然高額支出在短期內可能對自由現金流造
成暫時性壓抑，但隨著基礎建設規模擴大，預期將帶動獲利自2028 年起加速成長；亞馬遜亦設定於
2029 年達成 1 兆美元的營收目標。
而 5 月以來的財報數據也都顯示 ，AI 資本支出除了算力晶片之外，也進一步擴散至連接端與製造
設備端。其中輝達單季營收將挑戰 780 億至 800 億美元。毛利預計將穩定維持在 75%左右的高檔。
思科單季營收年增 12%至 158.4 億美元 ， 淨利年增35%至 33.7 億美元 。 受惠於大型雲端業者對基礎
建設的投入，思科將全年 AI 相關訂單預估由原本的 50 億美元大幅上調至 90 億美元。應用材料第 2
季營收達 79.1 億美元，獲利超市場預期，且第3 季財測達 89.5 億美元。執行長預期 2026 年設備業

務將成長三成以上，這些公司的財報都顯示 AI 投資由算力端向連接端及設備端外溢的趨勢明確，良
好的基本面已經成為科技類股的成長基石。
市場正擔憂債券殖利率走高與高利率環境對科技股估值造成的壓力，但我們認為，應從估值面與
企業獲利週期進行評估。當前的總體經濟與市場結構與 2021 年的緊縮週期有些類似，但存在本質上
的差異。在 2021 年前後，聯準會為快速飆升的通膨而啟動升息，科技股因而下跌，但當時那斯達克
100 指數的預估本益比高達 35 倍，且企業獲利正處於下行週期。而目前那斯達克 100 指數預估本益
比約僅為 25.6 倍低於當時，且企業獲利更明確處於上升週期。短線上，殖利率維持高檔確實會讓科
技股產生技術性的獲利了結與波動，但在長期 AI 資本支出結構性成長與整體企業獲利步入上升週期
的雙重支撐下 ， 高利率環境對科技股長線評價的威脅將逐漸緩解。 建議投資人關注整體產業獲利全面
提升的帶來的結構性機會。

台灣股市：先進製程帶動結構性擴張，類股良性輪動構築中長線多頭
台灣 4 月外銷訂單金額達 874.5 億美元，創歷年同月新高，年增率高達 48.1%。受到 AI、高效
能運算及雲端資料服務需求的強勁拉動 ， 其中電子產品與資訊通信產品訂單表現尤為顯著 ， 預期未來
數月外銷訂單可望穩定維持在每月 800 億美元的水準。在供應鏈「去中化」趨勢下，全球高端製造
需求正高度向台灣集中 。 半導體持續擴產亦帶動本土精密機械與檢測設備訂單增長 ， 動能已逐步外溢
至傳統產業，使整體經濟走向更全面的復甦。
在外銷訂單高度成長的背後，正是因為台積電在先進製程與封裝技術上確立了絕對領先地位帶動

了整個台灣上下游產業鏈。台積電技術論壇揭示，在 AI 驅動下，2030 年全球半導體市場規模將達
1.5 兆美元，其中 AI 將貢獻 55%以上的營收。目前全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）對輝達 Blackwell
架構的強烈需求，高度仰賴台積電先進封裝產能的釋放。台積電的 2 奈米（N2）製成即將量產，更
先進的製程預計於 2026 年下半年投產，並著手規劃埃米世代製程。反觀英特爾與三星目前仍在先進
製程節點與良率之間掙扎，台積電不僅在技術進程上領先一至兩個世代，更透過良率高達 98%的
CoWoS 先進封裝建立技術壁壘，短期內對手在技術成熟度、量產規模與客戶信任度上皆難以競爭。
基本面的強勁表現反映在台股的籌碼與盤勢結構上 。 隨著5 月 21 日輝達財報化解市場觀望心理 ，
三大法人全面翻多，單日合計買超達 850.51 億元，其中外資單日買超即佔了 603.41 億元，成交值
顯著放大 。 此波修正成功洗淨市場的短線浮額 。 資金從漲多的記憶體板塊撤出 ， 流入基期較低的面板、
載板、 被動元件等產業， 盤面呈現健康且偏多的類股輪動特徵 。 資金不再僅僅獨攬於少數AI 權值股，

而是向各受惠供應鏈上下游擴散，構築了多頭的廣度。
在地緣政治上，5 月川習會整體會談並無重大協議，中美脫鉤的大趨勢持續。中美雙方在台灣問
題上維持各自表述，中方警告「台獨」與台海和平水火不容；美方則重申對台政策維持不變，雙方沒
有重大改變。在全球 CSP 廠商對算力需求尚未觸頂的基本面下，台灣高端製造與半導體供應鏈的核
心地位 ， 並未受到政治會談結果的實質干擾 。 輝達財報行情與訂單表現依然反映出算力稀缺的市場共
識，地緣政治風險僅止於情緒面擾動，未動搖產業趨勢。 台灣在全球 AI 與高效能運算資本支出浪潮
中，憑藉半導體先進製程與封裝技術，展現出極強的定價權與不可替代性。這使得總體經濟指標表現
亮眼，且擴張動能正由科技業外溢至傳統產業。台股呈現健康的類股輪動格局，地緣政治政治雜音並
未動搖高端製造向台灣集中的趨勢，中長線維持看多格局。

拉美市場：巴西總統大選前料將震盪，後市中性看待
5 月以來拉美股市表現相對疲弱，主因隨著 10 月巴西總統大選逐步逼近，選情不確定性升溫，
導致最大經濟體巴西股市出現震盪，同時美伊和談狀況膠著，油價持續維持高檔，帶動整體拉美國家
通膨預期升溫，降息預期縮窄，股市上漲動能便受到壓抑。
回顧歷史，巴西股市於大選前半年走勢普遍不佳，過去五次選舉前六個月MSCI 巴西指數報酬中
位數為負 4%，其中 2014 年與 2022 年兩次大選年更以全年下跌作收，顯示選情雜音升高之際，投
資人多於投票前轉趨觀望。在大選過後，巴西股市報酬率則取決於是否由資本市場偏好的候選人當

選，若是，則股市大多上漲，若否，則股市大多下跌，巴西股市 5 月來的跌幅就與此相關。
本次巴西總統大選中，相對受到資本市場偏好的候選人波索納洛，近期捲入醜聞，導致其支持度
大幅滑落，其被指控與涉案巴西史上最大銀行詐欺案 Banco Master 的前執行長關係密切，並涉及收
受其資金用於拍攝家族紀錄片。受此影響，民調機構 AtlasIntel 5 月 19 日最新民調顯示，在第二輪
對決調查中，魯拉以 48.9%支持度領先波索納洛的 41.8%，波索納洛由原先小幅領先轉為落後約 7
個百分點。由於市場對偏左派的魯拉財政與政策路線疑慮較深，民調再度倒向魯拉，連帶導致巴西股
市表現承壓。
另一方面，受美伊戰爭影響，西德州原油價格持續維持高檔，帶動巴西輸入性通膨壓力上升，市
場對通膨回落的信心隨之轉弱。根據巴西央行截至 5 月 22 日的 Focus 市場調查，市場預期 2026 年
消費者物價指數預期中位數已由四週前的 4.86% 升至 5.04%，進一步突破 4.50%的通膨目標上緣；
受此影響，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 基準利率的預期亦由四週前的 13.00%上修至 13.25%，意味市場

預期下半年降息幅度僅約 1 個百分點，降息預期幅度持續收窄，連帶限制股市評價向上修正的空間。
然而儘管受到選舉及通膨升溫兩股承壓力量，高盛最新報告仍對於巴西股市中長線維持加碼評
等，看多理由在於，巴西股市對本地利率的負相關程度為新興市場中之最，在過去降息循環中，股市
於首次降息後往往出現強勁漲勢，而目前巴西市場仍處於降息循環中；此外，今年以來外資對巴西股
市淨流入居新興市場之冠，亦有利股市支撐。
展望後市，降息循環有利巴西股市支撐，然而考量巴西股市於大選前表現普遍不佳，同時油價高
檔推升通膨預期、降息幅度持續收窄的情況下，指數上檔空間恐受壓抑，故我們對於拉美市場後市目
前抱持中性看法。

能源類股：雖仍供不應求，惟油價料將隨談判進度震盪走低
當前油價與能源類股表現仍取決於美伊和談進展， 5 月 23 日川普宣稱一份結束戰事並重啟荷
莫茲海峽的備忘錄已大致談定，將採先確立大方向原則，後續再就細節分軌協商的模式來結束戰爭，
伊朗官媒指出，荷莫茲海峽通行與核子相關議題，將分別在備忘錄簽署後 30 天及 60 天進行談判，
談判期間美國將分階段豁免伊朗制裁。然而截至最新消息，美伊雙方在荷莫茲海峽主權與通行收費、
鈾濃縮是否完全停止等核心議題上仍存在明顯分歧，故該份備忘錄能否順利簽署仍具不確定性。
受到荷莫茲海峽持續封鎖影響，IEA（國際能源署）5 月月報指出，4 月全球石油供給量再度下

降每日 180 萬桶至每日 9,510 萬桶，下降主因波斯灣國家停產量持續上升，不過美國、巴西、加
拿大等國家持續增加出口，也為全球油市提供部分緩解，IEA 將 2026 年美洲供給成長上修逾每日
60 萬桶至平均每日 150 萬桶，大西洋盆地原油出口也自 2 月以來增加每日 350 萬桶。
此外全球石油需求量下滑，也部分抵銷供給缺口。IEA 將 2026 年全球石油需求下修至年減每
日 42 萬桶，較開戰前的預測減少了每日 130 萬桶，產業面看以石化與航空產業需求下滑幅度最
深，國家別中，中國 4 月海運原油進口量相較 2 月大幅減少每日 360 萬桶，日韓印亦分別縮減每日
190 萬、100 萬與 76 萬桶。全球石油需求下降，主因中東石油供給受阻，導致部分石化公司被迫
減產，此外部分也跟中國經濟表現疲弱，導致的需求下滑有關。
然而儘管全球石油需求量下滑 ， 非中東地區國家也持續增產 ， 全球石油市場仍面臨每日約750 萬
桶的供給缺口，受此影響， IEA 統計全球可見庫存 3 月減少 1.29 億桶，4 月再減 1.17 億桶，

高盛則預估 5 月以來全球可見石油庫存以每日 870 萬桶的創紀錄速度被消耗。
但值得注意的是，儘管全球石油市場面臨嚴重供不應求，理應有利油價上漲，然而整個 5 月西
德州原油價格卻仍維持在每桶 80 至 110 美元區間震盪，並未進一步上漲。高盛指出，油價上漲受
阻，主因經濟合作暨發展組織(OECD)國家以每日 130 萬桶速度釋出戰略石油儲備，填補了部分缺
口，同時中國原油進口量自 2 月 27 日以來大減每日 430 萬桶，使市場上對於可售原油的爭奪趨
緩，連帶使油價漲幅受到受抑。
展望後市油價，EIA（美國能源資訊署）預估布蘭特原油價格 5、6 月均價將約為 106 美元，
隨後續海峽通行恢復，第四季油價可望回落至每桶 89 美元，惟若海峽重開延後至六月底，則後續
油價將可能較此預測在上升每桶 20 美元。整體而言，後市油價及能源類股表現，仍將視美伊談判
進度而定，然而考量美伊戰事持續朝收斂方向前進，預料後續油價將隨談判進度震盪走低，相較不利
能源類股表現，在缺乏進一步利多支撐的狀況下，我們對於能源類股後市目前保守看待。

黃金類股：黃金表現仍承壓，惟全球央行買盤漸強
5 月受到美伊戰爭帶動美債殖利率上行影響，黃金表現仍承壓，然而我們觀察到來自全球央行的
買盤逐漸轉為強勁，顯示各國央行對於逢低增持黃金的興趣仍存，有利為金價帶來支撐，預料來自央
行的買盤將可使金價保持在區間內震盪整理，等待美伊衝突緩解，殖利率上行的壓力消退。
央行買盤方面，高盛於5 月報告大幅上修其央行購金量預估，主因其推估模型自 2025 年 8 月起
系統性低估各國央行購金量，經參數修正後，高盛預估各國央行購金量 12 個月移動平均值由原本估
計的每月 29 公噸調升至每月 50 公噸，單月修正幅度更大，1 月由 12 公噸上修至 66 公噸、2 月由 2

公噸上修至 41 公噸，2026 全年央行購金量則預估為平均每月 60 公噸。
世界黃金協會數據亦呼應此預估，根據世界黃金協會統計 ，2026 年第 1 季全球央行淨購金達 244
公噸，季增 17%，超越前季與五年均值。在已申報買方中，波蘭央行 Q1 增持 31 公噸為最大買家，
烏茲別克央行則增持 25 公噸、哈薩克央行 12 公噸緊隨其後。此外中國人行持續增持黃金動作也未
停歇，在 4 月中國人行單月增持 8 公噸黃金，創下 2024 年 12 月以來單月新高，並且是連續第 18
個月增持黃金儲備，帶動其官方持有量上升至 2,322 公噸，占外匯儲備 9%。從以上數據可以看出，
各國央行在近幾個月金價走疲的階段，仍持續加大力度增持黃金儲備。
在黃金 ETF 資金面亦可見到轉折出現的跡象，繼 3 月黃金 ETF 出現顯著資金流出後，4 月全球
實物黃金 ETF 轉為出現 66 億美元的資金流入，所有地區在均呈正向流入，推動全球黃金 ETF 黃金持
有量從 3 月底約 4,092 公噸反彈 45 公噸至 4,137 公噸，達到歷史第三高 ， 僅略低於2/27 創下的 4,176

公噸紀錄，其中歐洲基金表現最強，4 月流入 37 億美元使年初至今累計由負轉正，主要受地緣政治
風險升溫、英國央行立場不如預期鷹派及本地股市回跌驅動。亞洲則延續第 8 個月淨流入趨勢，4 月
增加 18 億美元，其中香港受惠於新 ETF 產品掛牌上市錄得 7.32 億美元單月歷史新高，中國在地緣
緊張 、 殖利率下行及官方持續宣布增持黃金的背景下亦流入 4.98 億美元。由黃金 ETF 數據亦可看出，
市場資金對於逢低增持黃金配置的興趣正在上升。
展望後市，在國際地緣政治紛擾不斷的狀況下 ，仍可預期央行買盤與個人投資組合中分散風險的
需求可望延續，有利為金價帶來支撐，然而在高油價將可能使利率維持在高檔較久的環境下，黃金
ETF 資金流入恐難以重現 2025 年的強度，故預期中短線上金價將可能持續維持在區間內震盪，惟若
後續美伊談判推展順利，隨荷莫茲海峽開放，油價回落將有利市場降息預期回升，屆時搭配來自央行
的買盤，將有望再度重啟黃金新一輪多頭走勢。

不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
自 2/28 美伊戰事爆發並導致荷莫茲海峽封鎖以來 ， 全球金融市場經歷自 1970 年代以來最嚴重
的供給端通膨衝擊。西德州原油與布蘭特原油價格的大幅飆升，引發市場對於通膨，乃至於 Fed 利
率政策方向轉變的疑慮，成為推升美債殖利率的核心驅動引擎。
這場由地緣政治引發的能源危機，並非僅侷限於美國單一國家，而是全球公債市場的普遍現象。
由於全球能源報價以美元計價 ， 且原油實體供給短缺屬於全球性危機 ， 主要已開發國家的公債殖利率
呈現極高相關性的同步上揚 ， 其中又以日本與英國的公債殖利率升幅最大 。 日本 75% 能源依賴中東

供給，不論在物價上或是財政體質上面臨不小壓力。
我們預期 6 月市場的總經焦點將在華許首次主持的 6/17 FOMC 利率會議。4 月下旬在分析華許
在參議院聽證會的發言時 ， 便已提出其主張可能導致 「美債殖利率陡峭化」 與 「債市波動性恐將加大」
的想法。彭博 4/30~5/13 調查顯示，118 名受訪者中有 64% 預期華許上任後，美債利率將出現某
種形式的陡峭化，其中熊市陡峭化（長率升速>短率升速）是主流觀點(佔比近 30%)。未來若債券殖
利率曲線呈現陡峭化(不論是熊市、牛市或是扭轉陡峭化)，短中天期的債券殖利率表現都將相對優於
長天期債券，加上短天期債券的存續期間本就低於長天期公債，因此當債市震盪時，短天期債券價格
表現將優於長天期債券。
巴克萊近期研究也顯示，當聯邦基金利率在 2-5% 的區間內波動，平均約為 3.6% 時，30 年
期公債殖利率與聯邦基金利率之間的利差為 170 個基點。假設長期中性利率在 3-4%的區間內（如
聯準會點陣圖所示），30 年期公債殖利率應在 4.7-5.7%區間。而目前約 5%水準約在區間相對偏低

的水準。巴克萊認為，在美國經濟韌性的背景下，目前30 年期公債殖利率還不算高，考慮到當前的
財政狀況和異常的不確定性，投資人可能需要更高的期限溢酬。若此說法成真，相較於短期債券，長
天期債券殖利率上升幅度更高(價格下跌幅度更大)。
雖然 5 月至今股市有所表現，但在中東地緣政治談判反覆，能源通膨預期以及聯準會偏鷹派立場
的交互牽引下，債券市場表現卻相對震盪，30 年期美債殖利率甚至一度觸及 2007 年以來的高位。
近來市場定價邏輯高度聚焦於「通膨風險與聯準會政策路徑」 ，債券殖利率 6 月仍將持續面臨高檔震
盪的壓力。目前債券殖利率水準明顯高於其歷史均值，我們認為站在息收 (Carry)的角度，債券仍具
長線投資價值。此外，由於預期美債殖利率曲線趨向陡峭化，再加上長天期公債本就有較高的存續期
間，故就債券價格來看，短天期公債/投資級債券的表現料將優於長天期公債/投資級債券，因此我們
仍維持債券投資聚焦中短天期公債/投資級債的建議。

2026 獲利亮眼且擁高現金殖利率

永豐觀點 
2026 年獲利逆勢上揚且淡季運價緩跌有撐，擁8%現金殖利率。觀察12 月巴拿馬
運河水位。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：(1) 美洲持續暢旺到明年春節，亞洲進入旺季，運價淡季相對有撐，(2) 
2027 年新增運力被碼頭作業能量不易擴充而抵銷，實質有效運力可能低於市場預
期，(3) 高現金殖利率的保護，下檔有撐，維持買進，目標價由 256 元(0.9X2026 
BVPS)上調到 278 元(0.85X2027BVPS)。

長榮為全球第 7 大海運業者：長榮海運是全球第 7 大航商，6 月底集團運力 204
萬 TEU，242 艘船舶，市佔率 5.9%，2025 年底運力 195.6 萬 TEU(+11%YoY)，
2026-2027 運力分別為 207.1 萬 TEU(+5.9%YoY)和 241 萬 TEU(+15.9%YoY)，
待交船 72 艘約 93.5 萬 TEU。1H26 年運送 545 萬 TEU(+4.2%YoY)，平均運費
1034 美元/TEU(-7%YoY)，2025 年航線別美洲線佔 39%，歐洲線佔 28%、亞洲
線佔 18%，其他佔 15%。 
3Q 運價強勢 ，4Q 運價相對有撐 ：1H26 美洲線艙位成長 0.96%， 貨量成長5.1%，
歐洲線艙位成長 7.5%，貨量成長 12.2%。地緣政治風險導致供應鏈不穩旺季提前
運送而上漲， 亞洲重要港口碼頭使用率偏高， 天候因素容易導致碼頭作業效率低造
成塞港與巴拿馬運河水位降低等均影響有效運力，支撐美東線運價重回 1 萬美元
/FEU 關卡，10/8 巴拿馬運河水位 85.2 英尺(-1.93%YoY)較 9 月中略有回升，但
11 月底水位雖回升到 85.9 英尺但低於去年同期 3.4%，航商預期美洲線貨量持續
暢旺到農曆春節，觀察 2Q27 美國是否啟動升息循環影響貨量。

2026 年供過增長率最低的一年：Alphaliner 預估 2026 需求成長 2.5%， 供給成長
率 4.7%，2026 年船舶供給成長率是近幾年最低的，目前貨櫃船在手訂單1400 萬
TEU，未來三年面臨新船大量下水壓力，然而公司認為受到地緣政治影響船期不穩
定性高、碼頭使用率偏高易受突發事件導致塞港、2028 年美西港口工人合約到期
等均影響有效運力，即使紅海復航採逐班逐航線，影響也會被抵銷，因此2027 年
的名目供給能否 100%轉為實質運力有待考驗 。 目前全球運力因為碼頭作業能量受
限、氣候因素、繞道、降速等影響 11%運力。

TEU(+1%YoY)。

燃油成本的影響 ：新加坡船用燃油 3Q 月平均 574 美元/噸(-7%QoQ%+44%YoY)，
低硫油價格 3Q 月平均 798 美元/噸(+3%QoQ%,+58%YoY)，長榮安裝脫硫塔的
船舶超過 9 成，船齡相對年輕省油，2025 年燃油成本佔 21%，1H26 佔 22%，
2Q 反應油價上漲會提升。長榮 2025 年平均用油價格 485 美元/噸(-8.1%YoY)，
1-8M26 用油價格 581 美元/噸(+16.7%YoY)，1-8 月燃油成本提升到 23%。

亮眼 ， 營收1468 億元(+51%YoY)符合預期 ， 預估稅後獲利413 億元(+90%YoY)，

### Page 1

Nanya Technology

Upbeat 3Q margin/ASP, and 2027 guidance 
also optimistic: lift PO to NT$800

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 800.00 TWD | Price: 527.00 TWD

3Q results confirm super-cycle even with legacy DRAM 
Nanya Tech, a pure DRAM supplier, reported very strong 3Q results (sales: NT$134bn, 
+62% QoQ / +612% YoY; OPM: 80% - similar to big-3 DRAM makers’; net margin: 66%). 
The key contributor should be upbeat ASP (up 60%+ QoQ vs consensus of 30-40%). In 
fact, Nanya Tech had already reported more than doubled 1H ASP (60%+ QoQ for two 
consecutive quarters). Thus, Street expected limited QoQ price increase in 3Q (vs 1H).  
According to Nanya Tech President Pei Ing Lee, demand for legacy DRAM products 
(DDR3 & DDR4) remained strong across IT, industrial, and enterprise applications. Mgmt 
also said legacy DRAM accounted for 80%+ of 3Q sales (vs 10% range for DDR5). Net-
net, the 3Q results confirmed super-cycle even selling mainly legacy/conventional DRAM. 
Guidance broadly bullish; no sign of a price cut 
Lee provided bullish guidance (earnings call). What we learned: (1) rising 4Q ASP, (2) 
ongoing shortage of legacy DRAM through 2027 (no immediate demand shift from 
DDR4 to DDR5), (3) new fab mass production (2027-28), (4) significant FCF generation 
(with high margin profile) despite large capex spend (NT$70bn in 2026), (5) record-high 
net cash (NT$270bn in 3Q), (6) new node development (in-house) and migration (1c, 1d, 
1e) on track, and (7) new construction plan for 3D wafer packaging/testing facilities in 
Ping Tung Science Park, Taiwan. Overall, new guidance presents further growth (capacity 
expansion) and high margin profile (favorable ASP trend). 
Lift PO to NT$800 on revisions to 2027/28 EPS estimates 
We lift our PO from NT$750 to NT$800 using the same target P/E vs earlier (9x 2027-
28E EPS). Thus, our PO revision is mostly based on 7-8% revisions to 2027-28 EPS 
estimates. We assume 5%/1ppt higher sales/OPM vs our earlier forecasts given more 
favorable conventional DRAM ASP trend. To be conservative, we assume negative EPS 
growth for 2028 but it can turn positive if the ASP remains stable. We reiterate Buy.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Nanya Technology is an affiliate of Formosa Plastics Group. 
The firm manufactures and distributes DRAM chips. 
Management focuses on non-PC applications such as 
consumer, mobile, server, autos, etc. Key customers are 
based in China and Taiwan. Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron 
are major competitors.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on the DRAM industry's supercycle, 
which should warrant a high-margin profile for Nanya Tech 
even in the long term. Its 3Q26 results proved record-high 
revenue (NT$134bn, more than 5x higher than the previous 
peak cycle) and 80% operating margin. Management focus 
on long-term contracts, including DDR4 for eSSD or servers, 
should also act as a catalyst. Significantly rising net cash 
even after capex hike could also support higher dividend 
payouts.

BofA Memory Indicator 
Exhibit 25: The BofA Memory Indicator still presents super-cycle – much higher than 2017-18 
peak level; of course, it slightly declined to 179 in Aug-26 (due to lower YoY growth) after 
hitting peak of 190 in Mar/Apr-26, driven by exceptionally strong DRAM/NAND spot pricing, 
rising ASPs and billings, along with Korea’s ~200%+ export growth. 
BofA Memory Indicator – back to upturn level since Oct-2025 and hit highest peak in Mar/Apr-26 
(back-tested)

Note: Our indicator had previously been capped at ~140, but the recent unprecedented surge—driven by memory spot prices, ASPs, and 
billings—has led us to raise the ceiling (up to 240 levels) to reflect a more accurate relative comparison. This exceptional strength is 
further validated by strong earnings from memory chipmakers. 
Source: DRAMeXchange, WSTS, MoTIR Korea, BofA Global Research

fund. Back-tested performance depicts the theoretical (not actual) performance of a particular strategy over the time period indicated. 
No representation is being made that any actual portfolio is likely to have achieved returns similar to those shown herein.

performance results are created by applying an investment strategy or methodology to
historical data and attempts to give an indication as to how a strategy might have
performed during a certain period in the past if the product had been in existence during
such time. Back-tested results have inherent limitations including the fact that they are
calculated with the full benefit of hindsight, which allows the security selection
methodology to be adjusted to maximize the returns. Further, the results shown do not

reflect actual trading or the impact that material economic and market factors might
have had on a portfolio manager's decision-making under actual circumstances. Back-
tested returns do not reflect advisory fees, trading costs, or other fees or expenses.

### Page 16

Price objective basis & risk 
Nanya Technology (NNYAF) 
Our PO of NT$800 is derived from 9x 2027-28E P/E, which is c.30% below the long-
term historical average (13x during 2014-25), but near-50% above the previous upturn 
period (6x in 2014-18). Memory stocks usually trade at low valuation multiples during 
peak cycle due to downturn concerns. However, the current DRAM cycle looks 
structurally much better than ever and we expect 2027-28 earnings to exceed 2026 level 
due to the AI growth theme. This could lead to a re-rating (e.g., 9x P/E or higher), with 
solid earnings outlook.

Upside risks: DRAM price increases even after the 1H26 upturn, Big-3 memory 
chipmakers' significant supply cut for commodity DRAM due to HBM, and Nanya Tech's 
more successful ramp-up of DDR4 or DDR5 (better cost or margin improvement).

Downside risks: weaker legacy DDR4 demand (quicker shift to DDR5), and China's more 
successful memory production increase.

potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings ar e: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
CPO: Optical scale-up is getting closer to reality. A 64-XPU CPO-enabled
compute-rack demonstration highlighted the potential for direct optical links

between accelerators. As 200G/lane electrical channels approach physical
limitations, optical scale-up will become more important, particularly for customized
XPUs. However, pluggagle optics should retain scale-out volume leadership given
advantages in serviceability and cost. Packaging yield, fiber alignment, and reliability
remain key commercialization challenges.
Advanced packaging: Moving beyond 2.5D. MediaTek highlighted custom ASIC
designs approaching 10x reticle size, arguing that 3.5D integration could deliver

approximately 10x better die-to-die energy efficiency versus conventional 2.5D
interconnects. Management expects increasing adoption over the next 3-5 years.
MediaTek also emphasized custom HBM, proprietary die-to-die interconnects, and
DTCO optimization. We see structural opportunities for advanced packaging and
custom ASIC design services as system complexity increases (link).
Memory: Capacity matters alongside bandwidth. SK hynix introduced its MARS

disaggregated memory architecture, demonstrating a 3-5x improvement in time-to-
first-token through shared KV-cache access versus recomputation. Meanwhile, HBF
targets approximately half of HBM bandwidth but 14x higher capacity, with samples
expected in early 2028. Inference workloads will increasingly require heterogeneous
memory architectures encompassing HBM, DRAM, and NAND, potentially expanding
memory content beyond traditional GPU-attached HBM (link).

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Optical interconnect: CPO is moving toward rack-scale scale-up, while pluggables
retain a scale-out advantage.
Custom ASICs and packaging: MediaTek sees 3.5D gaining traction as 2.5D
approaches physical limits
Memory architecture: SK hynix targets inference capacity and KV-cache
bottlenecks through MARS arid HBF.
Power and cooling: Racks above 200kW accelerate 800V DC, vertical power

delivery and liquid cooling.
October 13, 2026 04:27 PM GMT

Power and thermal: The next system-level constraints. Demonstrations of 6kw-
class double-sided cold plates vertical power delivery, and advanced microchannel
cooling highlighted intensifying thermal challenges. The transition toward 800V DC
should reduce conversion losses and improve power density. Meanwhile, AMD's
Helion agentic Al rack architecture reinforces incremental CPU demand alongside
GPU infrastructure. Power delivery, advanced cooling, and system integration

capabilities to become increasingly important differentiators.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O or Over) - The stock's total return is expected to exceed the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry
team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E or Equal) - The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or

industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR) - Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's
industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U or Under) - The stock's total return is expected to be below the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry

team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

MediaTek

ASIC business expanding into Bytedance; 
potential high-end mobile gains in ‘27

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 4,475 TWD

Bytedance expanding ASIC TAM beyond Google/xAI 
Bloomberg reported on October 15th that Bytedance is close to an agreement to work 
with Mediatek on the ASIC design and turnkey service support following its collaboration 
with Broadcom and Qualcomm. This is consistent with our update in September that 
Mediatek could co-develop an AI inference chipset with Bytedance from 1H27 for tape 
out in 1H28 and see contribution from production in early ’29. Similar to Mediatek and 
xAI’s collaboration, we believe Bytedance would need the support on sourcing 
components (e.g. HBM and substrate) in addition to wafer manufacturing/packaging, 
lifting the dollar content vs. Google’s TPUs. Assuming 1mn unit demand annually & 
US$20-25k ASPs including advanced logic dies and HBM, the project could contribute 
US$20-25bn to Mediatek’s sales in ’29 (vs. BofA sales expectation on Mediatek  of 
US$82bn in ’28 with US$45bn from Google TPUv8/v9 and US$9bn from xAI’s 1-2 
quarter contribution). 
A modest tailwind in mobile as it gains share at Samsung 
The mobile chipset builds from Mediatek and Qualcomm in 2H26 are tracking below 
seasonal in 2H26 as the end demand is impacted by the elevated memory cost. While 
the market outlook looms into ’27 (vs. -25% Android unit demand in ’26), we expect 
Mediatek’s mobile business to fare better especially in the high-end segment where it 
could gain unit share by penetrating into Samsung’s flagship smartphones historically 
dominated by Qualcomm and Samsung’s own SoCs. Factoring 30% share in Samsung’s 
high-end mobile shipment of 30-35mn annual units and US$200+ chipset ASPs, it could 
be ~20% accretive to Mediatek’s mobile sales and ~5% of its total business from 2H27. 
EPS and valuation downside limited after recent pullback 
We maintain our Buy rating & NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E (implied 30x ASIC 
EPS + 10x merchant EPS). The share has been dragged by TPU competition concern in 
the near term but could be lifted by expanding ASIC projects and high-end mobile gains.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment

研 究  報 告                
貿聯-KY(3665.TT) 
產業：電子–其他電子 
正式合併 Interplex 財報，大幅挹注 HPC 產品營收 
 投資評價與建議 
正式併購Interplex，預估2026/2027年稅後EPS為70.58/128.86元，過往貿聯
本益比位於17~30倍區間，考量併購與光通訊專案逐步發酵，給予25倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收：近期貿聯正式合
併Interplex集團資料通訊業務，並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。  
Interplex產品包含：伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，併購有利提
升貿聯在單⼀機櫃之產值，另外有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。 
HPC和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎 ， 光通訊佈局逐步發酵 ：展望2026~2027
年 ， 營收動能主要來自 ： (1)Ethernet AEC規格逐漸從400G往800G迭代 ， 800G 
AEC主要採用客戶為xAI與AWS，1.6T AEC預計將於4Q26隨Nvidia Rubin新
平台開始正式出貨，ALC主動式光纜目前已有2個以上客戶表示願意採用，預
估2H27開始貢獻營收 ； (2)Vera Rubin機櫃功率突破300kW ， 帶動Power Whip
規格從60A提升至100A 、 機櫃內Busbar規格從1,400A提升至5,000Ａ ， 電源規
格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra機櫃Power Rack設
計帶動Power Whip用量與ASP皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑2倍以上 ； (4)貿聯與
ams OSRAM合作開發microVCSEL互連方案，另外與SENKO和ficonTEC合作
全自動化光學互連與光纖製備流程，目前光通訊營收已佔整體營收約2~3%。 
 投資風險 
伺服器新平台出貨進度不如預期、原物料成本上漲。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
2Q26毛利率重回成⻑軌道，HPC與半導體設備需求持續強勁，3Q26營收創高：2Q26營收季增11.6%至232.6億元，係
因HPC與半導體設備業務需求持續強勁，1Q26毛利率僅28.8%，主要受車用等非AI低毛利產品復甦導致產品組合改變
以及xAI因併購使其資料中心建置進度停滯 ， 導致延後拉貨AEC與power產品 ， 貿聯產能利用率下降亦影響毛利率 ， 2Q26
毛利率季增1.96ppts至30.7%，重回過往水準，係因客戶逐步恢復拉貨，稅後EPS為15.28元。貿聯8月營收月減7.7%至
88.9億元，係因歐洲地區歷經季節性暑期⻑假，9月營收月增5%至93.3億元，營收達成率101%，符合預期，係因xAI逐
步恢復拉貨以及AWS Trainium 3放量，3Q26營收季增19.8%至278.9億元，預估稅後EPS為19.12元。 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收： 貿聯-KY於2026年6月宣布斥資8.5億美元收購新加坡
Interplex集團資料通訊業務，近期正式合併並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。Interplex營運總部位於新加坡，產能
遍及南亞與亞洲地區，包括：泰國、越南、⾺來⻄亞與中國等，其核心技術為精密金屬加工與機構件，主要產品包含：
伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，另外僅Busbar產品線與貿聯重疊且同為Nvidia MGX合格供應商，併購除擴
張HPC營收規模外，亦提升貿聯在單⼀AI Server機櫃之產值，另外透過併購有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。
2025年Interplex營收約4億美元，佔貿聯2025年營收之17%，隨AI運算與推論需求增加，假設Interplex 2026和2027年
營收皆年增30%，預估可貢獻貿聯2026/2027年營收分別約39與200億元。 
HPC 和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎，光通訊佈局逐步發酵，成為「銅光並進」主要受惠者：展望 2026~2027 年，貿
聯營收動能主要來自：(1)隨 AI 頻寬需求增加，Ethernet AEC 規格逐漸從 400G 往 800G 迭代，預計 2026 年底前成為
主流規格，800G AEC 主要採用客戶為 xAI 與 AWS，其中 xAI 因 AEC 採用規格最高且⻑度最⻑，故 ASP 較高，1.6T 
AEC 預計將於 4Q26 隨 Nvidia Rubin 新平台開始正式出貨，由於 1.6T AEC 採用 8 條單通道 200G 之線材組裝，故組
裝難度提升且成本翻倍，因此 ASP 和毛利率皆優於過往規格，另外 PCIe AEC 則已開發完成，將隨 CSP 推出 PCIe 版本
Switch 放量，ALC 主動式光纜目前已有 2 個以上客戶表示願意採用，預估 2H27 開始貢獻營收；(2)隨 Vera Rubin 機櫃
功率突破 300kW，帶動 Power Whip 規格從 60A 提升至 100A、機櫃內 Busbar 規格從 1,400A 提升至 5,000Ａ，電源
規格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra 機櫃可容納 576 顆 GPU 使單⼀機櫃功耗超過 500kW．由
於機櫃無法容納電源設備，故單獨拉出成為 Power Rack．帶動「Power Rack 至運算機櫃」電源連接需求大幅增加，預
估 Power Whip 之用量與 ASP 皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑超過 2 倍以上 ； (4)光通訊領域方面 ， 近期貿聯與 ams OSRAM
合作開發 AI scale-up microVCSEL 互連方案，另外持續與 SENKO 和 ficonTEC 合作全自動化的光學互連與光纖製備流
程，目前光通訊營收已佔公司整體營收約 2~3%，顯示光通訊產品成⻑⼒道強勁。綜上所述，預估 2026/2027 年營收
年增 49.5%/55.4%至 1,064.9/1,654.6 億元，稅後 EPS 為 70.58/128.86 元。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트HD현대중공업(329180) HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-11
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업은 전일(9/10) 대출력 발전엔진 (20MW, 9MW 급) 및 SMR 캐파 
확장을 위한 신규시설투자를 공시했다. 규모는 총 1조 722억원이며, 그 중 엔진 관련 
투자는 8,336억원, SMR 관련 투자는 2,386억원이다.

엔진 관련 투자에서는 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 확장을 위한 신규 공장 신설 및 기존 
공장 생산 설비 구축을 진행하고자 한다. 동사의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파는 기존 3GW 
규모이며, 이번 증설을 통해 신규 공장 캐파 3GW, 기존 공장 증설분 1.2GW가 
더해져 2028년 5월 기준 최종적으로 7.2GW 규모가 될 것으로 예상된다. 영암 
공장과 신축 공장에서는 데이터센터향으로 납품될 9MW 및 20MW 규모의 대출력 
엔진 위주, 기존 울산 공장은 3~5MW 규모의 선박용 엔진 위주의 생산이 이루어질 
계획이다. 이번 공장 신축 완료 이후에도 가용 부지가 남아있어 시장 수요에 따라 
향후 추가적인 캐파 확대 역시 고려할 수 있다.

SMR 투자의 경우 현대건설/테라파워와의 협력을 위한 SMR 주기기 연 2기 생산 
캐파 확보를 목적으로 진행되었다. 1기 당 출력 용량은 345MW이며, 구체적인 생산 
단가 및 물량의 경우 테라파워사와 지속 협의 중으로 동사의 미래 먹거리로서 
중장기적인 성장성에 기여할 전망이다.

데이터센터향 수요 성장에 대한 확신은 실제 엔진 업체들의 수주 인콰이어리 및 신규 
투자에서 드러나고 있다. 엔진 발전 시장 상위 업체 바르질라와 에버런스가 올해 
결정한 증설 규모는 총 4.5GW로 파악되며, 동사 역시 같은 행보로 신규 시장에서 
유의미한 점유율을 확보할 것으로 기대된다. 동사의 데이터센터향 4행정 중속엔진 
수주 단가를 고려했을 때 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 가능하다. 또한, 현재 동사 
엔진사업부의 캐시카우를 담당하고 있는 2행정 저속엔진 대비 좋은 수익성을 보여줄 
것으로 기대되는 바, 데이터센터향 생산이 시작될 28년 하반기부터 점진적으로 엔진 
믹스 개선 및 마진 성장이 가속화될 것으로 전망한다.

질문 내용 
1) 준공 이후 램프업 소요 기간 28 년 5 월 준공 이후 실질적 운영은 6 월 시작 
  28 년 30%, 29 년 60% 30 년 100% 가동 목표 
  4Q28 신공장 첫 엔진 생산 가능 예상 
2) 데이터센터향 엔진 수주 인콰이어리 지속 증가 추세, 8/10 수주 이후에도 추가 증가 중 
  인콰이어리가 모두 수주로 이어지진 않지만 증설 결정에는 충분한 수요라는 판단 
3) 발전용 엔진 인콰이어리 믹스 신규 인콰이어리 기준 육상용과 FDC 향 6:4~5:5 비율 
4) 경쟁사 대비 캐파 및 라인업 비교 바르질라, 에버런스가 발전용 엔진 시장 상위기업 
  양사 합산 증설 규모 4.5GW 추정,  
  경쟁사와의 라인업은 동사와 유사, 대출력 엔진 9MW, 20MW 급 위주 
5) 공장별 혼용 생산 가능 여부 기존 울산 공장 테스트 베드는 3-5MW 급 선박용에 최적화로 선박용 생산에 집중 계획 
  영암 공장 및 신규 공장은 모든 종류 엔진 생산 가능 
6) 바르질라 AM 사업과의 수익성 비교 바르질라와 유사하게 LTSA(장기유지보수계약) 구조로 진행될 가능성 높음 
  수익성 역시 바르질라와 유사한 수준 기대

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses

Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.

have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively

US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

Document 274: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 26-30

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30
Date:    39 minutes ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 26-30
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 24 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.5% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 0.7%, both
posting second straight weekly losses. The Nasdaq’s 2.9% slide on
Thursday was its biggest one-day drop since June, as concerns flared
over the cost and availability of financial capital for AI investment. The
price of WTI crude topped $100 a barrel amid US-Iran conflict concerns,
while the 10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.0%.
The FOMC meeting (Wed) is the focus this week, alongside the release of

Q3 GDP and September's PCED inflation report (Thu). San Francisco Fed
President Mary Daly and St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem speak
on Friday. A heavy week of Q3 earnings reports rounds out a packed
calendar. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC. The FOMC’s two-day meeting concludes Wednesday, followed
by Fed Chair Kevin Warsh’s press conference. The CME FedWatch Tool
puts the odds of a hike this week at 9% and the odds of one in December
at 84%. Federal funds futures imply 2.7 rate hikes over the next 12

months and 1.4 over the next six (chart). We believe the committee should
hike on Wednesday but won't because the midterm elections are so close.

### Page 4

(2) GDP & PCED. The first estimate of Q3 GDP (Thu) follows Q2’s third
estimate of 2.2% saar, with the GDP price index up 6.1%. The Atlanta
Fed’s GDPNow model estimates Q3 growth at 3.5% as of October 20, led
by a 17.8% jump in business equipment spending (chart).
September’s core PCED (Thu) follows August’s 3.0% y/y. That was well
below September’s 4.6% pace of core PPI final demand for personal
consumption and above the core CPI’s 2.6% (chart). The Cleveland Fed’s

Inflation Nowcasting model projects September’s core and headline PCED
at 3.1% and 3.6% y/y, up from August’s 3.0% and 3.4%. (The model’s
m/m rates are 0.28% and 0.47%.)
CPI computer software and accessories rose 28.4% y/y in September and
PPI electronic components 26.8%, while PCED computers and peripheral

### Page 5

equipment rose just 8.4% in August. September’s PCED will show
whether that gap starts to close.
(3) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a drop in the four-week average to 18,500 for the week
ended October 3, down from 26,000 the prior week (chart). That’s still
more than double July’s low of 8,250, pointing to a solid October payrolls
report.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 194,000, below expectations

and near the lowest level since the late 1960s. The four-week average
stands at 197,800 (chart). Continuing claims rose to 1,728,000 for the
week ended October 9, up from 1,710,000, while their four-week average
edged down to 1,708,000.

### Page 6

(4) Regional business surveys. The Richmond Fed’s manufacturing
index (Tue) follows September’s -2 reading. The regional M-PMI business
activity index slipped to 13.4 in September from August’s 18.7, while the
national M-PMI eased to 54.5 from 54.6 (chart).
(5) Earnings. The earnings reporting season heats up this week, with
36% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Boeing, Seagate, and
Corning report on Tuesday. Alphabet, Microsoft, SK Hynix, and GE

Vernova follow on Wednesday. Samsung, Intel, Eli Lilly, SanDisk, and
Amazon report on Thursday. Exxon Mobil reports on Friday.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 24.1% y/y, slightly up from last week (chart). Q4
estimates have also risen.

### Page 7

(6) Global central banks. Both the Reserve Bank of New Zealand and
the Bank of Canada (Wed) are expected to hike by 25bps. The markets
are pricing in 88% odds that the RBNZ will lift its official cash rate to
3.00% and an 85% chance that the BOC will raise its policy rate to 2.50%.
The European Central Bank (Thu) and the Bank of Japan (Fri) are set to
hold their policy rates at 2.50% and 1.25%, respectively (chart).
December is the focus for the latter pair. The markets are pricing in 75%

odds of an ECB hike in December and a 74% probability of a BOJ hike.
Guidance from ECB President Christine Lagarde and BOJ Governor
Kazuo Ueda will show whether those odds hold.

Document 289: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom
Cookie settings

preferences here at any time.

Conﬁrm selection Accept all

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising
Cookie settings
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
Here you can specify which type of cookies you consent to and
which you prefer to disable. Once you've made your selection,
please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie
preferences here at any time.
Read more about our Cookie policy
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

Cookie settings
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
Here you can specify which type of cookies you consent to and
which you prefer to disable. Once you've made your selection,
please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie
preferences here at any time.
Read more about our Cookie policy
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled
Cookie settings

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

the major taxes, which together account for around 70% of total
revenue.
Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.
How the Chancellor could raise taxes

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

The chart below shows that in April, the Debt Management Oﬃce
projected a gross ﬁnancing need of £308bn, similar to the big
number in FY2025. We suspect that when we get the updated DMO
remit following the budget, next year’s issuance could be closer to
£325bn – though a greater chunk of that will likely get funded using
bills, meaning the actual pick-up in gilt sales relative to those
previous plans is likely to be more modest.

Still, the point is that, beneath these numbers, public sector net
borrowing is still likely to be projected to keep falling through the
rest of the decade. The major risk – related to some of the things

discussed earlier, like larger near-term spending or surprise
increases in tax allowances – would jeopardise this fundamental
improvement in bond issuance in the medium term. That's
something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

金融市場波動上升，增配低波、防禦價值股更穩健

### Page 8

8
► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。
